Mathlib.Topology.Category.TopPair

TopPairdefMathlib.Topology.Category.TopPair, type
Wording
top pair (from the name)
English
A top pair is an arrow of the top and the top.
Formula
$\mathrm{TopPair} := \operatorname{Arrow} (\operatorname{isEmbedding} , \operatorname{top} , \operatorname{top})$
French
Une paire supérieure est une flèche du plus grand élément et le plus grand élément.
TopPair.Hom.fstdefMathlib.Topology.Category.TopPair, function or constant
Wording
the map between the first spaces of f (from the docstring)
English
Let $f$ be a homomorphism of $X$ and $Y$. Then the map between the first spaces of $f$ is a homomorphism of the first space of the pair and the first space of the pair defined as the right of the homomorphism of $f$.
Formula
$f \in \operatorname{Hom} (X , Y)\vdash \operatorname{fst} (f):= \operatorname{right} (\operatorname{hom} (f))\in \operatorname{Hom} (\operatorname{fst} , \operatorname{fst})$
French
Soit $f$ un homomorphisme de $X$ et $Y$. Alors le [DepNoun] de $f$ est un homomorphisme du [DepNoun] premier et le [DepNoun] premier défini comme le droit de l'homomorphisme de $f$.

Wording

English
Let $f$ be a homomorphism of $X$ and $Y$. Then the map between the second spaces of $f$ is a homomorphism of the second space of the pair and the second space of the pair defined as the left of the homomorphism of $f$.
Formula
$f \in \operatorname{Hom} (X , Y)\vdash \operatorname{snd} (f):= \operatorname{left} (\operatorname{hom} (f))\in \operatorname{Hom} (\operatorname{snd} , \operatorname{snd})$
French
Soit $f$ un homomorphisme de $X$ et $Y$. Alors le [DepNoun] de $f$ est un homomorphisme du [DepNoun] second et le [DepNoun] second défini comme la gauche de l'homomorphisme de $f$.
TopPair.HomotopicdefMathlib.Topology.Category.TopPair, predicate
Wording
f is homotopic to g (from the name)
English
Let $f$ and $g$ be homomorphisms of $X$ and $Y$. Then $f$ is homotopic to $g$, if $\mathrm{Nonempty}$ holds for the set of homotopies of $f$ and $g$.
Formula
$f , g \in \operatorname{Hom} (X , Y)\vdash \operatorname{Homotopic} (f , g):\Leftrightarrow \mathrm{Nonempty} (\operatorname{Homotopy} (f , g))$
French
Soient $f$ et $g$ des homomorphismes de $X$ et $Y$. Alors $f$ est homotopique à $g$ si $\mathrm{Nonempty}$ tient pour l'ensemble d'homotopies de $f$ et $g$.
TopPair.HomotopystructureMathlib.Topology.Category.TopPair, type
Wording
homotopy of f and g (from the name)
English
Let $f$ and $g$ be homomorphisms of $X$ and $Y$. Then the homotopies of $f$ and $g$ are a basic type.
Formula
$f , g \in \operatorname{Hom} (X , Y)\vdash \operatorname{Homotopy} (f , g)\ \mathsf{set}$
French
Soient $f$ et $g$ des homomorphismes de $X$ et $Y$. Alors les homotopies de $f$ et $g$ sont un type de base.
TopPair.Homotopy.compdefMathlib.Topology.Category.TopPair, function or constant
Wording
the composition of G and F (from the name)
English
Let $G$ be a homotopy of $x_{2}$ and $x_{3}$. Let $F$ be a homotopy of $x$ and $x_{1}$. Then the composition of $G$ and $F$ is a homotopy of the composition of $x$ and $x_{2}$ and the composition of $x_{1}$ and $x_{3}$.
Formula
$G \in \operatorname{Homotopy} (x_{2} , x_{3}), F \in \operatorname{Homotopy} (x , x_{1})\vdash \operatorname{comp} (G , F)\in \operatorname{Homotopy} (\operatorname{comp} (x , x_{2}), \operatorname{comp} (x_{1} , x_{3}))$
French
Soit $G$ une homotopie de $x_{2}$ et $x_{3}$. Soit $F$ une homotopie de $x$ et $x_{1}$. Alors la composition de $G$ et $F$ est une homotopie de la composition de $x$ et $x_{2}$ et la composition de $x_{1}$ et $x_{3}$.
TopPair.Homotopy.fstdefMathlib.Topology.Category.TopPair, function or constant
Wording
the homotopy on the first space of self (from the docstring)
English
Let $\mathrm{self}$ be a homotopy of $f$ and $g$. Then the homotopy on the first space of $\mathrm{self}$ is a homotopy of the map between the first spaces of $f$ and the map between the first spaces of $g$ defined as $\operatorname{proj0}$ applied to $\mathrm{self}$.
Formula
$\mathrm{self} \in \operatorname{Homotopy} (f , g)\vdash \operatorname{fst} (\mathrm{self}):= \operatorname{proj0} (\mathrm{self})\in \operatorname{Homotopy} (\operatorname{fst} (f), \operatorname{fst} (g))$
French
Soit $\mathrm{self}$ une homotopie de $f$ et $g$. Alors le [DepNoun] de $\mathrm{self}$ est une homotopie du [DepNoun] de $f$ et le [DepNoun] de $g$ défini comme $\operatorname{proj0}$ appliqué à $\mathrm{self}$.

TopPair.ofdefMathlib.Topology.Category.TopPair, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $f$ be a homomorphism of $A$ and $X$ such that $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to the homomorphism of $f$ is an embedding. Then $\operatorname{of}$ applied to $f$ and $h$ is a top pair defined as $\operatorname{mk}$ applied to $f$ and $h$.
Formula

French

TopPair.ofHomdefMathlib.Topology.Category.TopPair, function or constant
Wording
the top pair of f and g (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a homomorphism of the first space of the pair and the first space of the pair. Let $g$ be a homomorphism of the second space of the pair and the second space of the pair such that $\mathrm{autoParam}$ holds for the proposition that the composition of $g$ and the map is equal to the composition of the map and $f$ and $\mathrm{TopPair\_\_ofHom\_\_\_auto\_1}$. Then the top pair of $f$ and $g$ is a homomorphism of $X$ and $Y$.
Formula
$f \in \operatorname{Hom} (\operatorname{fst} , \operatorname{fst}), g \in \operatorname{Hom} (\operatorname{snd} , \operatorname{snd}), \mathrm{autoParam} (\EqtMACRoJ {\operatorname{comp} (g , \operatorname{map})}{\operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{map} , f)}, \mathrm{TopPair\_\_ofHom\_\_\_auto\_1})\vdash \operatorname{ofHom} (f , g , w)\in \operatorname{Hom} (X , Y)$
French
Soit $f$ un homomorphisme du [DepNoun] premier et le [DepNoun] premier. Soit $g$ un homomorphisme du [DepNoun] second et le [DepNoun] second tel que $\mathrm{autoParam}$ tienne pour la proposition que la composition de $g$ et l'application est égale à la composition de l'application et $f$ et $\mathrm{TopPair\_\_ofHom\_\_\_auto\_1}$. Alors la paire supérieure de $f$ et $g$ est un homomorphisme de $X$ et $Y$.

Wording
S is a compact system (from the docstring)

C (i))}{\operatorname{emptyCollection}}\rightarrow (\exists n \in \Natural . \EqtMACRoJ {\operatorname{dissipate} (C , n)}{\operatorname{emptyCollection}})$

IsLindelofdefMathlib.Topology.Compactness.Lindelof, predicate
Wording
s is a Lindelof (from the name)
English
Let $s$ be an element of $\wp X$. Then $s$ is a Lindelof, if for all filters $f$ of $X$, if $\operatorname{NeBot}$ holds for $f$, then if $f$ is a countable intersection filter, then if $f$ is less than or equal to the principal filter of $s$, then there exists an element $x_{1}$ of $X$, such that $x_{1}$ is an element of $s$ and $\mathrm{ClusterPt}$ holds for $x_{1}$ and $f$.
Formula
$s \in \wp X \vdash \mathrm{IsLindelof} (s):\Leftrightarrow \forall f \in \mathrm{Filter} (X). \operatorname{NeBot} (f)\rightarrow \mathrm{CountableInterFilter} (f)\rightarrow f \leq \operatorname{principal} (s)\rightarrow (\exists x_{1} \in X . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {s}{x_{1}}\wedge \mathrm{ClusterPt} (x_{1} , f))$
French
Soit $s$ un élément de $\wp X$. Alors $s$ est un Lindelof si pour tous les filtres $f$ de $X$, si $\operatorname{NeBot}$ tient pour $f$, alors si $f$ est un filtre d'intersections dénombrable, alors si $f$ est inférieur ou égal au filtre principal de $s$, alors il existe un élément $x_{1}$ de $X$ tel que $x_{1}$ soit un élément de $s$ et $\mathrm{ClusterPt}$ tienne pour $x_{1}$ et $f$.

IsSigmaCompactdefMathlib.Topology.Compactness.SigmaCompact, predicate
Wording
s is a sigma compact (from the name)
English
Let $s$ be an element of $\wp X$. Then $s$ is a sigma compact, if there exists a function $K$ from natural numbers to elements of $\wp X$, such that for all natural numbers $n$, $K$ applied to $n$ is compact and the union of the function that maps $n_{1}$ to $K$ applied to $n_{1}$ is equal to $s$.
Formula
$s \in \wp X \vdash \mathrm{IsSigmaCompact} (s):\Leftrightarrow \exists K \in \Natural \rightarrow \wp X . (\forall n \in \Natural . \mathrm{IsCompact} (K (n)))\wedge (\EqtMACRoJ {\operatorname{iUnion} (\lambda n_{1} . K (n_{1}))}{s})$
French
Soit $s$ un élément de $\wp X$. Alors $s$ est un compact de sigmas s'il existe une fonction $K$ des nombres naturels à des éléments de $\wp X$ tel que pour tous des nombres naturels $n$, $K$ appliqué à $n$ soit compact et l'union de la fonction qui envoie $n_{1}$ à $K$ appliqué à $n_{1}$ soit égale à $s$.

IsPreconnecteddefMathlib.Topology.Connected.Basic, predicate
Wording
s is preconnected (from the name)
English
Let $s$ be an element of $\wp x$. Then $s$ is preconnected, if for all elements $u$ and $v$ of $\wp x$, if $u$ is open, then if $v$ is open, then if $s \leq \UnionunionMACRoAB {u}{v}$, then if $\operatorname{inter}$ applied to $s$ and $u$ is nonempty, then if $\operatorname{inter}$ applied to $s$ and $v$ is nonempty, then $\operatorname{inter}$ applied to $s$ and $\operatorname{inter}$ applied to $u$ and $v$ is nonempty.
Formula
$s \in \wp x \vdash \mathrm{IsPreconnected} (s):\Leftrightarrow \forall u \in \wp x . \forall v \in \wp x . \mathrm{IsOpen} (u)\rightarrow \mathrm{IsOpen} (v)\rightarrow s \leq \UnionunionMACRoAI {u}{v}\rightarrow \operatorname{Nonempty} (\operatorname{inter} (s , u))\rightarrow \operatorname{Nonempty} (\operatorname{inter} (s , v))\rightarrow \operatorname{Nonempty} (\operatorname{inter} (s , \operatorname{inter} (u , v)))$
French
Soit $s$ un élément de $\wp x$. Alors $s$ est préconnexe si pour tous les éléments $u$ et $v$ de $\wp x$, si $u$ est ouvert, alors si $v$ est ouvert, alors si $s \leq \UnionunionMACRoAB {u}{v}$, alors si $\operatorname{inter}$ appliqué à $s$ et $u$ est non vide, alors si $\operatorname{inter}$ appliqué à $s$ et $v$ est non vide, alors $\operatorname{inter}$ appliqué à $s$ et $\operatorname{inter}$ appliqué à $u$ et $v$ est non vide.

Set.SeparatesPointsStronglydefMathlib.Topology.ContinuousMap.Algebra, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $s$ be an element of the power set of the set of continuous maps of $x$ and $x_{2}$. Then $\operatorname{SeparatesPointsStrongly}$ holds for $s$, if for all functions $v$ from elements of $x$ to elements of $x_{2}$, for all elements $x_{4}$ and $y$ of $x$, there exists a continuous map $f$ of $x$ and $x_{2}$, such that $f$ is an element of $s$, $\EqtMACRoH {f (x_{4})}{v (x_{4})}$ and $\EqtMACRoH {f (y)}{v (y)}$.
Formula
$s \in \wp \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (x , x_{2})\vdash \operatorname{SeparatesPointsStrongly} (s):\Leftrightarrow \forall v \in x \rightarrow x_{2} . \forall x_{4} \in x . \forall y \in x . \exists f \in \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (x , x_{2}). \MembershipmemMACRoAA {s}{f}\wedge (\EqtMACRoJ {f (x_{4})}{v (x_{4})}\wedge (\EqtMACRoJ {f (y)}{v (y)}))$
French
Soit $s$ un élément de la puissance de l'ensemble d'applications continues de $x$ et $x_{2}$. Alors $\operatorname{SeparatesPointsStrongly}$ tient pour $s$ si pour toutes les fonctions $v$ des éléments de $x$ à des éléments de $x_{2}$, pour tous les éléments $x_{4}$ et $y$ de $x$, il existe une application continue $f$ de $x$ et $x_{2}$ tel que $f$ soit un élément de $s$, $\EqtMACRoH {f (x_{4})}{v (x_{4})}$ et $\EqtMACRoH {f (y)}{v (y)}$.

Wording
no wording

$s \in \mathrm{Subalgebra} (R , \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (x , A))\vdash \operatorname{SeparatesPoints} (s):\Leftrightarrow \operatorname{SeparatesPoints} (\operatorname{image} (\lambda f . \operatorname{coe} (f), s))$

Let $x$ and $x_{1}$ be sets. Then the continuous subgroup of $x$ and $x_{1}$ is a subgroup of the set of functions from elements of $x$ to elements of $x_{1}$.
Formula
$x , x_{1} \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \mathrm{continuousSubgroup} (x , x_{1})\in \mathrm{Subgroup} (x \rightarrow x_{1})$
French
Soient $x$ et $x_{1}$ des ensembles. Alors le sous-groupe continu de $x$ et $x_{1}$ est un sous-groupe de l'ensemble de fonctions des éléments de $x$ à des éléments de $x_{1}$.
continuousSubmoduledefMathlib.Topology.ContinuousMap.Algebra, function or constant
Wording
the continuous submodule of R and M (from the name)
English
Let $x$, $R$ and $M$ be sets. Then the continuous submodule of $R$ and $M$ is a submodule of $R$ and the set of functions from elements of $x$ to elements of $M$.
Formula
$x , R , M \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \mathrm{continuousSubmodule} (x , R , M)\in \mathrm{Submodule} (R , x \rightarrow M)$
French
Soient $x$, $R$ et $M$ des ensembles. Alors le sous-module continu de $R$ et $M$ est un sous-module de $R$ et l'ensemble de fonctions des éléments de $x$ à des éléments de $M$.

ContinuousMap.compdefMathlib.Topology.ContinuousMap.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the composition of f and g (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a continuous map of $x_{1}$ and $x_{2}$. Let $g$ be a continuous map of $x$ and $x_{1}$. Then the composition of $f$ and $g$ is a continuous map of $x$ and $x_{2}$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (x_{1} , x_{2}), g \in \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (x , x_{1})\vdash \operatorname{comp} (f , g)\in \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (x , x_{2})$
French
Soit $f$ une application continue de $x_{1}$ et $x_{2}$. Soit $g$ une application continue de $x$ et $x_{1}$. Alors la composition de $f$ et $g$ est une application continue de $x$ et $x_{2}$.

French

ContinuousMap.liftCoverdefMathlib.Topology.ContinuousMap.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the lift cover of S and x5 (from the name)
English
Let $S$ be a function from elements of $x_{4}$ to elements of $\wp x$. Let $x_{5}$ be a function from elements $i_{2}$ of $x_{4}$ to continuous maps of the set of elements of $S$ applied to $i_{2}$ and $x_{1}$ such that for all elements $i_{1}$ and $j$ of $x_{4}$, for all elements $x_{7}$ of $x$, for all elements $\mathrm{hxi}$ of $\mathrm{Proof}$ applied to $\MembershipmemMACRoT {S (i_{1})}{x_{7}}$, for all elements $\mathrm{hxj}$ of $\mathrm{Proof}$ applied to $\MembershipmemMACRoT {S (j)}{x_{7}}$, $\EqtMACRoH {x_{5} (i_{1} , x_{7})}{x_{5} (j , x_{7})}$. Assume that for all elements $x_{6}$ of $x$, there exists an element $i$ of $x_{4}$, such that $S$ applied to $i$ is an element of the neighbourhood filter of $x_{6}$. Then the lift cover of $S$ and $i_{2}$ is a continuous map of $x$ and $x_{1}$.
Formula
$S \in x_{4} \rightarrow \wp x , x_{5} \in x_{4} \rightarrow \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (\operatorname{Elem} (S (i_{2})), x_{1}), \forall i_{1} \in x_{4} . \forall j \in x_{4} . \forall x_{7} \in x . \forall \mathrm{hxi} \in \mathrm{Proof} (\MembershipmemMACRoAA {S (i_{1})}{x_{7}}). \forall \mathrm{hxj} \in \mathrm{Proof} (\MembershipmemMACRoAA {S (j)}{x_{7}}). \EqtMACRoJ {x_{5} (i_{1} , x_{7})}{x_{5} (j , x_{7})}, \forall x_{6} \in x . \exists i \in x_{4} . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {\mathrm{nhds} (x_{6})}{S (i)}\vdash \operatorname{liftCover} (S , i_{2} , x_{5} , h , \mathrm{hS})\in \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (x , x_{1})$
French
Soit $S$ une fonction des éléments de $x_{4}$ à des éléments de $\wp x$. Soit $x_{5}$ une fonction des éléments $i_{2}$ de $x_{4}$ à des applications continues de l'ensemble d'éléments de $S$ appliqué à $i_{2}$ et $x_{1}$ telle que pour tous les éléments $i_{1}$ et $j$ de $x_{4}$, pour tous les éléments $x_{7}$ de $x$, pour tous les éléments $\mathrm{hxi}$ de $\mathrm{Proof}$ appliqué à $\MembershipmemMACRoT {S (i_{1})}{x_{7}}$, pour tous les éléments $\mathrm{hxj}$ de $\mathrm{Proof}$ appliqué à $\MembershipmemMACRoT {S (j)}{x_{7}}$, $\EqtMACRoH {x_{5} (i_{1} , x_{7})}{x_{5} (j , x_{7})}$. Supposons que pour tous les éléments $x_{6}$ de $x$, il existe un élément $i$ de $x_{4}$ tel que $S$ appliqué à $i$ soit un élément du filtre de voisinages de $x_{6}$. Alors le recouvrement de relèvements de $S$ et $i_{2}$ est une application continue de $x$ et $x_{1}$.

no wording

Wording

English

Then the product equivalence of $A$ and $X$ is an equivalence from the set of functions from elements $i$ of $I$ to continuous maps of $A$ and $X$ applied to $i$ to the set of continuous maps of $A$ and the set of functions from elements $i_{1}$ of $I$ to elements of $X$ applied to $i_{1}$.

ContinuousMap.restrictPreimagedefMathlib.Topology.ContinuousMap.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the restriction of f and s (from the docstring)
English
Let $f$ be a continuous map of $x$ and $x_{1}$. Let $s$ be an element of $\wp x_{1}$. Then the restriction of $f$ and $s$ is a continuous map of the set of elements of the preimage of $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $f$ and $s$ and the set of elements of $s$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (x , x_{1}), s \in \wp x_{1} \vdash \operatorname{restrictPreimage} (f , s)\in \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (\operatorname{Elem} (\operatorname{preimage} (\operatorname{coe} (f), s)), \operatorname{Elem} (s))$
French
Soit $f$ une application continue de $x$ et $x_{1}$. Soit $s$ un élément de $\wp x_{1}$. Alors la restriction de $f$ et $s$ est une application continue de l'ensemble d'éléments de la préimage de $\operatorname{coe}$ appliqué à $f$ et $s$ et l'ensemble d'éléments de $s$.
ContinuousMap.sigmadefMathlib.Topology.ContinuousMap.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the sigma of f (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a function from elements $i_{1}$ of $I$ to continuous maps of $X$ applied to $i_{1}$ and $A$. Then the sigma of $i_{1}$ is a continuous map of $\Sigma$ applied to the function that maps $i$ to $X$ applied to $i$ and $A$.
Formula
$f \in I \rightarrow \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (X (i_{1}), A)\vdash \operatorname{sigma} (i_{1} , f)\in \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (\Sigma (\lambda i . X (i)), A)$
French
Soit $f$ une fonction des éléments $i_{1}$ de $I$ à des applications continues de $X$ appliqué à $i_{1}$ et $A$. Alors le sigma de $i_{1}$ est une application continue de $\Sigma$ appliqué à la fonction qui envoie $i$ à $X$ appliqué à $i$ et $A$.
ContinuousMap.sigmaEquivdefMathlib.Topology.ContinuousMap.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the sigma equivalence of A and X (from the name)
English
Let $A$ be a set. Let $X$ be a function from elements of $I$ to sets. Then the sigma equivalence of $A$ and $X$ is an equivalence from the set of functions from elements $i$ of $I$ to continuous maps of $X$ applied to $i$ and $A$ to the set of continuous maps of $\Sigma$ applied to the function that maps $i_{1}$ to $X$ applied to $i_{1}$ and $A$.
Formula
$A \in \mathrm{Set} , X \in I \rightarrow \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{sigmaEquiv} (A , X)\in \EquivMACRoAL {(\mathrm{Elem} (I)\rightarrow \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (X (i), A))}{\mathrm{ContinuousMap} (\Sigma (\lambda i_{1} . X (i_{1})), A)}$
French
Soit $A$ un ensemble. Soit $X$ une fonction des éléments de $I$ à des ensembles. Alors l'équivalence de sigmas de $A$ et $X$ est une équivalence de l'ensemble de fonctions des éléments $i$ de $I$ à des applications continues de $X$ appliqué à $i$ et $A$ à l'ensemble d'applications continues de $\Sigma$ appliqué à la fonction qui envoie $i_{1}$ à $X$ appliqué à $i_{1}$ et $A$.

Wording
the lift of g (from the name)
English
Assume that $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $f$ is a quotient map. Let $g$ be a continuous map of $X$ and $Z$ such that $\operatorname{FactorsThrough}$ holds for $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $g$ and $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $f$. Then the lift of $g$ is a continuous map of $Y$ and $Z$.
Formula
$\operatorname{IsQuotientMap} (\operatorname{coe} (f)), g \in \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (X , Z), \operatorname{FactorsThrough} (\operatorname{coe} (g), \operatorname{coe} (f))\vdash \operatorname{lift} (\mathrm{hf} , g , h)\in \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (Y , Z)$
French

Soit $g$ une application continue de $X$ et $Z$ telle que $\operatorname{FactorsThrough}$ tienne pour $\operatorname{coe}$ appliqué à $g$ et $\operatorname{coe}$ appliqué à $f$. Alors le relèvement de $g$ est une application continue de $Y$ et $Z$.

Wording
no wording
English

Soit $f$ une application continue de $x$ et $x_{1}$. Soit $x_{4}$ un nombre réel tel que $0 < x_{4}$. Alors le module de $f$ et $x_{4}$ est un nombre réel défini comme $\operatorname{choose}$ appliqué à $\mathrm{ContinuousMap\_\_uniform\_continuity}$ appliqué à $f$, $x_{4}$ et $h_{1}$.

Mathlib.Topology.ContinuousMap.Defs

ContinuousMapstructureMathlib.Topology.ContinuousMap.Defs, type
Wording
continuous map of X and Y (from the name)
English
Let $X$ and $Y$ be sets. Then the continuous maps of $X$ and $Y$ are a basic type.
Formula
$X , Y \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (X , Y)\ \mathsf{set}$
French
Soient $X$ et $Y$ des ensembles. Alors les applications continues de $X$ et $Y$ sont un type de base.
ContinuousMap.copydefMathlib.Topology.ContinuousMap.Defs, function or constant
Wording
the copy of f and h (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a continuous map of $X$ and $Y$. Let $\mathrm{f'}$ be a function from elements of $X$ to elements of $Y$ such that $\mathrm{f'}$ is equal to $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $f$. Then the copy of $f$ and $\mathrm{f'}$ is a continuous map of $X$ and $Y$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (X , Y), \mathrm{f'} \in X \rightarrow Y , \EqtMACRoJ {\mathrm{f'}}{\operatorname{coe} (f)}\vdash \operatorname{copy} (f , \mathrm{f'} , h)\in \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (X , Y)$
French
Soit $f$ une application continue de $X$ et $Y$. Soit $\mathrm{f'}$ une fonction des éléments de $X$ à des éléments de $Y$ telle que $\mathrm{f'}$ soit égal à $\operatorname{coe}$ appliqué à $f$. Alors la copie de $f$ et $\mathrm{f'}$ est une application continue de $X$ et $Y$.

the polynomial function of X (from the name)
English
Let $X$ be an element of $\wp R$. Then the polynomial function of $X$ is a subalgebra of $R$ and the set of continuous maps of the set of elements of $X$ and $R$ defined as the map of the continuous map on algebra homomorphism of $X$ and the top.
Formula
$X \in \wp R \vdash \mathrm{polynomialFunctions} (X):= \operatorname{map} (\operatorname{toContinuousMapOnAlgHom} (X), \operatorname{top})\in \mathrm{Subalgebra} (R , \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (\operatorname{Elem} (X), R))$
French
Soit $X$ un élément de $\wp R$. Alors la fonction polynomiale de $X$ est une sous-algèbre de $R$ et l'ensemble d'applications continues de l'ensemble d'éléments de $X$ et $R$ défini comme l'application de l'homomorphisme d'applications sur de l'algèbre continu de $X$ et le plus grand élément.

Mathlib.Topology.ContinuousMap.Sigma

ContinuousMap.sigmaCodHomeomorphdefMathlib.Topology.ContinuousMap.Sigma, function or constant
Wording
the sigma codomain homeomorphism of X and Y (from the name)
English
Let $X$ be a set. Let $Y$ be a function from elements of $x$ to sets. Then the sigma codomain homeomorphism of $X$ and $Y$ is a homeomorphism of the set of continuous maps of $X$ and $\Sigma$ applied to the function that maps $i$ to $Y$ applied to $i$ and $\Sigma$ applied to the function that maps $i_{1}$ to the set of continuous maps of $X$ and $Y$ applied to $i_{1}$.
Formula
$X \in \mathrm{Set} , Y \in x \rightarrow \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{sigmaCodHomeomorph} (X , Y)\in \mathrm{Homeomorph} (\mathrm{ContinuousMap} (X , \Sigma (\lambda i . Y (i))), \Sigma (\lambda i_{1} . \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (X , Y (i_{1}))))$
French
Soit $X$ un ensemble. Soit $Y$ une fonction des éléments de $x$ à des ensembles. Alors l'homéomorphisme de codomaines de sigmas de $X$ et $Y$ est un homéomorphisme de l'ensemble d'applications continues de $X$ et $\Sigma$ appliqué à la fonction qui envoie $i$ à $Y$ appliqué à $i$ et $\Sigma$ appliqué à la fonction qui envoie $i_{1}$ à l'ensemble d'applications continues de $X$ et $Y$ appliqué à $i_{1}$.

Mathlib.Topology.Convenient.ContinuousMapGeneratedBy

Topology.ContinuousGeneratedBydefMathlib.Topology.Convenient.ContinuousMapGeneratedBy, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $X$ be a function from elements of $x$ to sets. Let $g$ be a function from elements of $Y$ to elements of $Z$. Then $\operatorname{ContinuousGeneratedBy}$ holds for $X$ and $g$, if for all elements $i$ of $x$, for all continuous maps $f$ of $X$ applied to $i$ and $Y$, $\operatorname{comp}$ applied to $g$ and $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $f$ is continuous.
Formula
$X \in x \rightarrow \mathrm{Set} , g \in Y \rightarrow Z \vdash \operatorname{ContinuousGeneratedBy} (X , g):\Leftrightarrow \forall i \in x . \forall f \in \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (X (i), Y). \mathrm{Continuous} (\operatorname{comp} (g , \operatorname{coe} (f)))$
French
Soit $X$ une fonction des éléments de $x$ à des ensembles. Soit $g$ une fonction des éléments de $Y$ à des éléments de $Z$. Alors $\operatorname{ContinuousGeneratedBy}$ tient pour $X$ et $g$ si pour tous les éléments $i$ de $x$, pour toutes les applications continues $f$ de $X$ appliqué à $i$ et $Y$, $\operatorname{comp}$ appliqué à $g$ et $\operatorname{coe}$ appliqué à $f$ est continu.
Topology.ContinuousGeneratedBy.continuousMapdefMathlib.Topology.Convenient.ContinuousMapGeneratedBy, function or constant
Wording
the continuous map of hg (from the name)
English
Assume that $\operatorname{ContinuousGeneratedBy}$ holds for $X$ and $g$. Then the continuous map of $\mathrm{hg}$ is a continuous map of $\operatorname{WithGeneratedByTopology}$ applied to $X$ and $Y$ and $\operatorname{WithGeneratedByTopology}$ applied to $X$ and $Z$.
Formula
$\operatorname{ContinuousGeneratedBy} (X , g)\vdash \operatorname{continuousMap} (\mathrm{hg})\in \mathrm{ContinuousMap} (\operatorname{WithGeneratedByTopology} (X , Y), \operatorname{WithGeneratedByTopology} (X , Z))$
French
Supposons que $\operatorname{ContinuousGeneratedBy}$ tient pour $X$ et $g$. Alors l'application continue de $\mathrm{hg}$ est une application continue de $\operatorname{WithGeneratedByTopology}$ appliqué à $X$ et $Y$ et $\operatorname{WithGeneratedByTopology}$ appliqué à $X$ et $Z$.
